 133: struct pcc2_data {
 134: 	struct interrupt	cpu_irq;
 135: 
 136: 	uint8_t			pcctwo_reg[PCC2_SIZE];
 137: 
 138: 	/*  pcc2widthkills: one bit per width-guard block, see the enum.  Zeroed by
 139: 	    the init memset, so every instance starts with every announce armed.  */
 140: 	uint32_t		warned;
 141: 	/*  #501: one bit per BYTE OFFSET (0..0x3f) for the default: arm's announce;
 142: 	    relative_addr is folded to PCC2_SIZE above the switch, so the shift is
 143: 	    always defined.  Zeroed by the same memset.  */
 144: 	uint64_t		warned_unimpl;
 145: 
 146: 	uint8_t			cur_int_vec[8];
 147: 
 148: 	/*  Timers:  */
 149: 	struct timer		*timer;
 150: 	int			pending_timer1_interrupts;
 151: 	int			pending_timer2_interrupts;
 152: };
 153: /*  pcc2widthkills: the enum must fit the field.  A seat pointed out that a bare
 154:     `<= 32' tested a literal, not the field, so narrowing `warned' would compile
 155:     silently; this is dev_dreamcast_gdrom.c's own form.  Negative array size is a
 156:     compile-time error, C99, no assert.h.  */
 157: typedef char pcc2_w_fits_in_warned[(PCC2_W_NBITS <= 8 * sizeof(((struct pcc2_data *)0)->warned)
 158:     && PCC2_W_NBITS <= 8 * sizeof(1u)) ? 1 : -1];
 159: 
 160: 
 161: static uint32_t read32(unsigned char *p)
 162: {
 163: 	uint32_t x = 0;
 164: 	size_t i;
 165: 
 166: 	for (i=0; i<sizeof(x); i++) {
 167: 		x <<= 8;
 168: 		x |= p[i];
 169: 	}
 170: 
 171: 	return x;
 172: }
 173: 
 174: 
 175: static void write32(unsigned char *p, uint32_t x)
 176: {
 177: 	size_t i;
 178: 
 179: 	for (i=0; i<sizeof(x); i++) {
 180: 		p[sizeof(x) - 1 - i] = x;
 181: 		x >>= 8;
 182: 	}
 183: }
 184: 
 185: 
 186: static void pcc_timer_tick(struct timer *t, void *extra)
 187: {
 188: 	struct pcc2_data *d = (struct pcc2_data *) extra;
 189: 
 190: 	/*  The PCC2 timers run at 1 MHz.  */
 191: 	int steps = (int) (1000000.0 / PCC_TIMER_TICK_HZ);
 192: 
 193: 	uint32_t count1, count2, compare1, compare2;
 194: 	int interrupt1 = 0, interrupt2 = 0;
 195: 
 196: 	/*  Read values:  */
 197: 	count1 = read32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1COUNT]);
 198: 	count2 = read32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2COUNT]);
 199: 	compare1 = read32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CMP]);
 200: 	compare2 = read32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CMP]);
 201: 
 202: 	/*  Timer enabled? Then count...  */
 203: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] & PCC2_TCTL_CEN) {
 204: 		uint32_t old_count1 = count1;
 205: 		count1 += steps;
 206: 
 207: 		/*  Did we pass the compare value?  */
 208: 		if ((old_count1 < compare1 && count1 >= compare1) ||
 209: 		    (old_count1 < compare1 && count1 < old_count1)) {
 210: 			interrupt1 = 1;
 211: 
 212: 			if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] & PCC2_TCTL_COC) {
 213: 				while (count1 >= compare1) {
 214: 					count1 -= compare1;
 215: 					interrupt1 ++;
 216: 				}
 217: 				interrupt1 --;
 218: 			}
 219: 		}
 220: 
 221: 		/*  Overflow?  */
 222: 		if ((int32_t) old_count1 >= 0 && (int32_t) count1 < 0) {
 223: 			int oc = 1 + (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] >> 4);
 224: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] &= ~PCC2_TCTL_OVF;
 225: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] |= (oc << 4);
 226: 		}
 227: 	}
 228: 
 229: 	/*  ... and the same for timer 2:  */
 230: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CTL] & PCC2_TCTL_CEN) {
 231: 		uint32_t old_count2 = count2;
 232: 		count2 += steps;
 233: 
 234: 		if ((old_count2 < compare2 && count2 >= compare2) ||
 235: 		    (old_count2 < compare2 && count2 < old_count2)) {
 236: 			interrupt2 = 1;
 237: 			if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CTL] & PCC2_TCTL_COC) {
 238: 				while (count2 >= compare2) {
 239: 					count2 -= compare2;
 240: 					interrupt2 ++;
 241: 				}
 242: 				interrupt2 --;
 243: 			}
 244: 		}
 245: 
 246: 		if ((int32_t) old_count2 >= 0 && (int32_t) count2 < 0) {
 247: 			int oc = 1 + (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CTL] >> 4);
 248: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CTL] &= ~PCC2_TCTL_OVF;
 249: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CTL] |= (oc << 4);
 250: 		}
 251: 	}
 252: 
 253: 	/*  Should we cause interrupts?  */
 254: 	if (interrupt1 && d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IEN)
 255: 		d->pending_timer1_interrupts += interrupt1;
 256: 	if (interrupt2 && d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IEN)
 257: 		d->pending_timer2_interrupts += interrupt2;
 258: 
 259: 	/*  Write back values:  */
 260: 	write32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1COUNT], count1);
 261: 	write32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2COUNT], count2);
 262: }
 263: 
 264: 
 265: static void reassert_interrupts(struct pcc2_data *d)
 266: {
 267: 	int assert = 0;
 268: 
 269: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_GENCTL] & PCC2_C040) {
 270: 		/*  The M68000 interrupt mechanism involves outputting
 271: 		    interrupt level on pins EIPL<2..0>. Not implemented
 272: 		    yet.  */
 273: 		fatal("pcc2: C040 interrupt assertions... TODO\n");
 274: 		exit(1);
 275: 	}
 276: 
 277: 	/*
 278: 	 *  Recalculate current interrupt level:
 279: 	 */
 280: 	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = 0;
 281: 
 282: 	/*  Timer interrupts?  */
 283: 	if (d->pending_timer1_interrupts > 0 &&
 284: 	    d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IEN)
 285: 		d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1ICR] |= PCC2_TTIRQ_INT;
 286: 	if (d->pending_timer2_interrupts > 0 &&
 287: 	    d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IEN)
 288: 		d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2ICR] |= PCC2_TTIRQ_INT;
 289: 
 290: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_INT) {
 291: 		int intlevel = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IL;
 292: 		d->cur_int_vec[intlevel] = PCC2V_TIMER1;
 293: 		if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] < intlevel)
 294: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = intlevel;
 295: 	}
 296: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_INT) {
 297: 		int intlevel = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IL;
 298: 		d->cur_int_vec[intlevel] = PCC2V_TIMER2;
 299: 		if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] < intlevel)
 300: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = intlevel;
 301: 	}
 302: 
 303: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCTX] & PCC2_IRQ_IEN &&
 304: 	    d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCTX] & PCC2_IRQ_INT) {
 305: 		int intlevel = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCTX] & PCC2_IRQ_IPL;
 306: 		d->cur_int_vec[intlevel] = PCC2V_SCC_TX;
 307: 		if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] < intlevel)
 308: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = intlevel;
 309: 	}
 310: 
 311: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCRX] & PCC2_IRQ_IEN &&
 312: 	    d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCRX] & PCC2_IRQ_INT) {
 313: 		int intlevel = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCRX] & PCC2_IRQ_IPL;
 314: 		d->cur_int_vec[intlevel] = PCC2V_SCC_RX;
 315: 		if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] < intlevel)
 316: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = intlevel;
 317: 	}
 318: 
 319: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCSIICR] & PCC2_IRQ_IEN &&
 320: 	    d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCSIICR] & PCC2_IRQ_INT) {
 321: 		int intlevel = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCSIICR] & PCC2_IRQ_IPL;
 322: 		d->cur_int_vec[intlevel] = PCC2V_SCSI;
 323: 		if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] < intlevel)
 324: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = intlevel;
 325: 	}
 326: 
 327: 	/*  TODO: Other interrupt sources.  */
 328: 
 329: 	/*  Assert interrupt on the CPU if the IPL is higher than the mask:  */
 330: 	if ((d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] & INTERRUPT_LEVEL_MASK) >
 331: 	    (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_MASK] & INTERRUPT_LEVEL_MASK))
 332: 		assert = 1;
 333: 
 334: 	debug("[ pcc2: IPL = %i, MASK = %i => %s ]\n", d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL]& INTERRUPT_LEVEL_MASK,
 335: 		d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_MASK] & INTERRUPT_LEVEL_MASK,
 336: 		assert ? "ASSERT" : "no assert");
 337: 
 338: 	/*  ... but only allow interrupts if Master Interrupt Enable is on:  */
 339: 	if (!(d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_GENCTL] & PCC2_MIEN))
 340: 		assert = 0;
 341: 
 342: 	if (assert)
 343: 		INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->cpu_irq);
 344: 	else
 345: 		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->cpu_irq);
 346: }
 347: 
 348: 
 349: void pcctwo_interrupt_common(struct interrupt *interrupt, int assert)
 350: {
 351: 	struct pcc2_data *d = (struct pcc2_data *) interrupt->extra;
 352: 
 353: 	switch (interrupt->line) {
 354: 
 355: 	case PCC2V_SCSI:
 356: 		if (assert)
 357: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCSIICR] |= PCC2_IRQ_INT;
 358: 		else
 359: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCSIICR] &= ~PCC2_IRQ_INT;
 360: 		break;
 361: 
 362: 	case PCC2V_SCC_TX:
 363: 		if (assert)
 364: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCTX] |= PCC2_IRQ_INT;
 365: 		else
 366: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCTX] &= ~PCC2_IRQ_INT;
 367: 		break;
 368: 
 369: 	case PCC2V_SCC_RX:
 370: 		if (assert)
 371: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCRX] |= PCC2_IRQ_INT;
 372: 		else
 373: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCRX] &= ~PCC2_IRQ_INT;
 374: 		break;
 375: 
 376: 	default:fatal("[ pcctwo_interrupt_common: TODO: line = %i ]\n",
 377: 		    interrupt->line);
 378: 		exit(1);
 379: 	}
 380: 
 381: 	reassert_interrupts(d);
 382: }
 383: 
 384: 
 385: static void pcctwo_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
 386: {
 387: 	pcctwo_interrupt_common(interrupt, 1);
 388: }
 389: static void pcctwo_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
 390: {
 391: 	pcctwo_interrupt_common(interrupt, 0);
 392: }
 393: 
 394: 
 395: DEVICE_TICK(pcc2)
 396: {
 397: 	struct pcc2_data *d = (struct pcc2_data *) extra;
 398: 	reassert_interrupts(d);
 399: }
 400: 
 401: 
 402: /*  pcc2widthkills: decline a wide WRITE to a byte register, announcing once per
 403:     block per instance.  The caller `break's after this; the read side of the same
 404:     block never reaches here.  */
 405: static void pcc2_width_decline(struct pcc2_data *d, int bit, const char *name, int len)
 406: {
 407: 	if (!(d->warned & (1u << bit))) {
 408: 		d->warned |= (1u << bit);
 409: 		fatal("[ pcc2: unimplemented len=%i write to %s -- declined."
 410: 		    "  (once) ]\n", len, name);
 411: 	}
 412: }
 413: 
 414: DEVICE_ACCESS(pcc2)
 415: {
 416: 	uint64_t idata = 0;
 417: 	struct pcc2_data *d = (struct pcc2_data *) extra;
 418: 
 419: 	/*  0xfff42000..0xfff42fff, but only 0x40 unique registers:  */
 420: 	relative_addr %= PCC2_SIZE;
 661: 		}
 662: 		break;
 663: 
 664: 	default:
 665: 		/*  #501 (pcc2defaultkill): this arm ended the host with exit(1) on ANY
 666: 		    access to an offset with no case -- 43 of the 0x40 byte offsets:
 667: 		    eighteen documented registers the model does not implement (IEICR,
 668: 		    IEERR, IEBERR, GPIO, SCSIERR, the printer block, SPEED, the *IACKs),
 669: 		    the bytes inside the four counter/compare words, and the unnamed
 670: 		    ones -- READS included, though the memcpy above the switch had
 671: 		    already served them.  A guest ld.bu or st.b at any of them was a host
 672: 		    kill (rung-3 witness).  Now: a READ keeps the served byte (the array's
 673: 		    default, said so -- not a computed value); a WRITE is DECLINED.  Not a
 674: 		    backed store: the cased siblings contradict one (SCCERR clears on
 675: 		    write, the ICRs mask and reassert, PSCALECNT/SPEED/the IACKs are
 676: 		    read-only), no guest reaches these offsets on this model (if_ie never
 677: 		    attaches: unreadable sits at its 82596 address), and a store from the
 678: 		    one arm 43 offsets reach would add a bounded-write surface.  One
 679: 		    latched fatal() per offset per instance, the house `(once)' shape --
 680: 		    unlike PSCALEADJ's UNLATCHED print four arms up (pcc2pscaleflood).  The
 681: 		    IACK offsets 0x23/0x25 are declined like the rest: modelling them (on
 682: 		    the SCCRXIACK shape) would open two new entries into the C040 path,
 683: 		    so pcc2c040kill goes first.  The %= fold above IS the real part's:
 684: 		    Motorola VMESBCP2 Table 6-1 lists $FFF42040-$FFF42FFF as repeated
 685: 		    PCCchip2 registers.  */
 686: 		if (!(d->warned_unimpl & (1ULL << relative_addr))) {
 687: 			d->warned_unimpl |= 1ULL << relative_addr;
 688: 			if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 689: 				fatal("[ pcc2: unimplemented write to offset "
 690: 				    "0x%x: 0x%x -- declined.  (once) ]\n",
 691: 				    (int) relative_addr, (int) idata);
 692: 			else
 693: 				fatal("[ pcc2: unimplemented read from offset "
 694: 				    "0x%x -- served 0x%02x.  (once) ]\n",
 695: 				    (int) relative_addr,
 696: 				    d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr]);
 697: 		}
 698: 		break;
 699: 	}
 700: 
 701: 	return 1;
 702: }
 703: 
 704: 
 705: DEVICE_ACCESS(mvme187_iack)
 706: {
 707: 	uint64_t odata = 0;
 708: 	struct pcc2_data *d = (struct pcc2_data *) extra;
 709: 
 710: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 711: 		fatal("[ pcc2: write to mvme187_iack? ]\n");
 712: 	} else {
 713: 		odata = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_VECBASE] + d->cur_int_vec[
 714: 		    (relative_addr >> 2) & INTERRUPT_LEVEL_MASK];
 715: 		debug("[ pcc2: mvme187_iack level %i => vector 0x%02x ]\n",
 716: 		    (int)(relative_addr >> 2), (int)odata);
 717: 
 718: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
 719: 	}
 720: 
 721: 	return 1;
 722: }
 723: 
 724: 
 725: DEVINIT(pcc2)
 726: {
 727: 	struct pcc2_data *d;
 728: 	int i;
 729: 
 730: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct pcc2_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct pcc2_data)));
 731: 	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct pcc2_data));
 732: 
 733: 	/*
 734: 	 *  Initial values, according to the manual:
 735: 	 *
 736: 	 *  VECBASE is 0x0f after a reset, although the lowest four bits
 737: 	 *  cannot be manually written to after startup.
 738: 	 */
 739: 	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_CHIPID] = PCC2_ID;
 740: 	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_CHIPREV] = 0x00;
 741: 	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_GENCTL] = 0x00;
 742: 	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_VECBASE] = 0x0f;
 743: 	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_PSCALEADJ] = 256 - 33;	// 256 - MHz (fake)
 744: 
 745: 	/*  Connect to the CPU's interrupt pin:  */
 746: 	INTERRUPT_CONNECT(devinit->interrupt_path, d->cpu_irq);
 747: 
 748: 	/*
 749: 	 *  Register the 16 PCC2 interrupt vectors:
 750: 	 */
 751: 	for (i=0; i<16; i++) {
 752: 		struct interrupt templ;
 753: 		char n[300];
 754: 		snprintf(n, sizeof(n), "%s.pcc2.%i",
 755: 		    devinit->interrupt_path, i);
 756: 
 757: 		memset(&templ, 0, sizeof(templ));
 758: 		templ.line = i;
 759: 		templ.name = n;
 760: 		templ.extra = d;
 761: 		templ.interrupt_assert = pcctwo_interrupt_assert;
 762: 		templ.interrupt_deassert = pcctwo_interrupt_deassert;
 763: 
 764: 		interrupt_handler_register(&templ);
 765: 	}
 766: 
 767: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "pcc2",
 768: 	    devinit->addr, 4096, dev_pcc2_access, (void *)d,
 769: 	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
 770: 
 771: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "mvme187_iack",
 772: 	    M187_IACK, 32, dev_mvme187_iack_access, (void *)d,
 773: 	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
 774: 
 775: 	d->timer = timer_add(PCC_TIMER_TICK_HZ, pcc_timer_tick, d);
 776: 
 777: 	machine_add_tickfunction(devinit->machine,
 778: 	    dev_pcc2_tick, d, DEV_PCC2_TICK_SHIFT);
 779: 
 780: 	return 1;
 781: }

 106:       REFUSAL-effect site, which a kill is not.  `d->warned' is a 32-bit field with twelve bits assigned.
 107: 
 108:       A NINTH SHAPE, on the input the six-shape list named and never walked: RDDECL, the
 109:       direction split's own conjunct nulled, `writeflag == MEM_WRITE' -> `<= MEM_WRITE'
 110:       (ONE character; MEM_READ is 0).  The Opus seat MEASURED it 82/82 on pass 3: wide
 111:       READS were declined, announced as writes, skipped the arm's read body and consumed
 112:       the block's latch bit -- and no session holding a wide read carried a decline-count
 113:       row.  Closed by `hrd <block> nod' / `wrd sccerr nod' on the SEVEN wide-read sessions
 114:       that already existed (six even blocks by ld.h, SCCERR by ld): a declined read is a
 115:       DECL where zero is expected.  The six ODD blocks' WIDE-read cells are UNREACHABLE by
 116:       construction -- a guest wide read at an odd offset alignment-faults before memory_rw
 117:       (BYTE reads do reach them; see THE SECOND PREDICATE),
 118:       and a debugger read does not dispatch to a DM_DEFAULT device -- and are disclosed,
 119:       not rowed.  One cosmetic gap, disclosed: the seven `op * dcl' rows count the GENERIC
 120:       decline text, and for FIVE arms (VECBASE, PSCALEADJ, SCCICR, SCCRX, SCSIICR) no other
 121:       row pins the name string, so a change of the NAME alone there is invisible; the
 122:       T1 labels share TxCTL/TxICR's fatal() with the named `rb t2ctl dcl' / `rb t2icr dcl'
 123:       rows, and `latch all' pins cardinality.
 124: 
 125:       THE SHARED BLOCKS.  TxCTL serves T1CTL (0x17, odd) and T2CTL (0x16, even); TxICR
 126:       serves T1ICR (0x1b) and T2ICR (0x1a).  The blocks' readbacks above are at the T2
 127:       offsets; a seat noted that a fall-through reaching only the T1 labels (the block
 128:       split in two) would leave `op t1ctl dcl' at 1 with nothing reading T1CTL back.  So
 129:       the T1 labels carry their own operator-path readbacks, `rb t1ctl' and `rb t1icr'.
 130:       THE THIRTEENTH ARM.  SCCRXIACK has NO width guard and never had one: its body
 131:       ignores `idata' (it clears a bit in SCCRX and reasserts), so its effect is
 132:       width-invariant by construction and it is not a member of the twelve.  It is named
 133:       here so the census is not read as an omission.
 134:       SESSIONS.  Every session() is a fresh pty.fork/execv of the emulator, so no latch
 135:       state survives from one row to the next; `latch all' counts one process.
 136:       THE HELPER'S OWN OPERATIONS (pass 4).  pcc2_width_decline() has two lines that
 137:       every block depends on: the TEST `!(d->warned & (1u << bit))' and the SET
 138:       `d->warned |= (1u << bit)'.  One-token edits to the SET line escaped every row:
 139:       `|=' -> `=' (assign: A, B clears A -- `latch aba' A,B,A = 2) and `(1u << bit)' ->
 140:       `1' (bit 0 always: every other block announces every time -- `latch same2', MASK
 141:       twice = 1).  The TEST line's operand swap (`&' -> `==') is caught by `latch all':
 142:       with the field accumulating, equality fails from the second block on and it
 143:       announces 12 either way; `latch aba' DOES distinguish it (A=1, then the field is 3,
 144:       and 3 == 1 is false, so A re-announces -- MEASURED 100/101 by a pass-5 seat).  An
 145:       earlier version of this sentence called it indistinguishable; the instrument was
 146:       stronger than its contract said.  `|=' -> `^=' or `+=' in the SET line is
 147:       EQUIVALENT: the SET runs only under the TEST, i.e. only when the bit is clear.
 148:       WIDTHS.  The debugger drives len 2 (`put h'), 4 (`put w') and 8 (`put d'); the
 149:       guest drives 1, 2 and 4.  `latch all', `latch all w' and `latch all d' pin the
 150:       ANNOUNCE at every block for each non-byte width, so a guard true on a subset of
 151:       {2,4,8} drops one of the three counts; the REFUSAL per width is `rb *', `rb4 *'
 152:       and `rb8 *' (see THE LANDING CHANNEL AT EVERY WIDTH).
 153:       BIT IDENTITY.  `latch all' and `latch all w' are cardinality rows: they assert
 154:       twelve announces, never WHICH bit.  A translation of the bit index
 155:       (`1u << bit' -> `1u << (bit + 1)') is EQUIVALENT by construction -- no code
 156:       outside pcc2_width_decline() reads d->warned's bits -- and was MEASURED 96/96 by a
 157:       pass-4 seat as the clause-(f) control.  That warrant expires the day anything
 158:       indexes or reports those bits.
 159:       ANNOUNCE SEVERITY.  Every row counts TEXT on a pty run under -V; `fatal(' ->
 160:       `debug(' in the helper changes no text these rows read.  NOT CAUGHT, disclosed;
 161:       the fork's recorded warrant for fatal() over debug() is dev_asc.c's (-q makes a
 162:       debug()-level line invisible to the boot-log hygiene grep).
 163:       THE FAULT MEMBER.  `break' -> `return 0' at a guard turns the decline into a bus
 164:       error.  At the six GUEST-reachable blocks the guest's store faults and the program
 165:       never prints, so the block's `lv' row reds while its `hwr' row stays green -- the
 166:       HOST survives (M_RET0_GENCTL, part 4: `hwr genctl lv' died, `hwr genctl' exit0; an
 167:       earlier version of this sentence and PRETABLE_pass4 said the pair).  At
 168:       the six OPERATOR-only blocks NOTHING above sees it -- MEASURED 101/101 at VECBASE
 169:       by a pass-5 seat -- so `latch all fail' counts memory_rw's "failed ]" line at zero
 170:       over the twelve-block session (M_RET0_VECBASE, part 5).
 171:       THE FIX'S OWN STATE AS A GUARD INPUT (pass 5, MEASURED 101/101 at one arm and at all
 172:       twelve by the Opus seat).  A guard qualified by its own latch bit -- `&& !(d->warned &
 173:       (1u << bit))', the canonical clause-(d) conjunct -- declines and announces the FIRST
 174:       wide write exactly as the fix does and lands the SECOND.  Every row above observed
 175:       the device only while its latch was still armed.  Closed at every block by `rb2 *'
 176:       (set, two wide writes, read back), `pscale noig2' and `ipl noig2' (the body's print,
 177:       counted at zero after two writes); SCCERR is value-identical there too (E).
 178:       THE SECOND PREDICATE.  Each arm's `if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)' is now load-bearing
 179:       for reads that fall through to it: WIDE reads at the even blocks since the split
 180:       (the pre-fix guard killed them first), and BYTE reads at EVERY block, always (a byte
 181:       access skips the alignment check and passes `len != 1').  Deleting it stores
 182:       idata == 0 on a read while the readback in the same stream -- served by the memcpy
 183:       before the switch -- still prints the old value; only a second read sees it.
 184:       Pinned by `hrd * rb' (wide, four even storing blocks), `hrd ipl noro', and by
 185:       `brd2 *' (byte, read twice, all nine storing blocks) with `brd pscale noig' /
 186:       `brd ipl noro'; SCCERR is E.  It does not dominate the edited lines, so it sits
 187:       outside the clause-(d) closure and is carried here as construction, not as a breach.
 188:       THE LANDING CHANNEL AT EVERY WIDTH (pass 6, Opus seat, measured).  `latch all',
 189:       `latch all w' and `latch all d' pin the ANNOUNCE at all twelve for len 2, 4, 8 --
 190:       not the refusal: `break;' -> `if (len < 4) break;' at all twelve announced and

 *  Read at most 64 bits of data from a buffer.  Length is given by
 *  len, and the byte order by cpu->byte_order.
 *
 *  This function should not be called with cpu == NULL.
 */
uint64_t memory_readmax64(struct cpu *cpu, unsigned char *buf, int len)
{
	int i, byte_order = cpu->byte_order;
	uint64_t x = 0;

	if (len & MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN) {
		len &= ~MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN;
		byte_order = EMUL_LITTLE_ENDIAN;
	}

	/*  Switch byte order for incoming data, if necessary:  */
	if (byte_order == EMUL_BIG_ENDIAN)
		for (i=0; i<len; i++) {
			x <<= 8;
			x |= buf[i];
		}
	else
		for (i=len-1; i>=0; i--) {
			x <<= 8;
			x |= buf[i];
		}

	return x;
}


/*
 *  memory_writemax64():
 *
 *  Write at most 64 bits of data to a buffer.  Length is given by
 *  len, and the byte order by cpu->byte_order.
 *
 *  This function should not be called with cpu == NULL.
 */
void memory_writemax64(struct cpu *cpu, unsigned char *buf, int len,
	uint64_t data)
{
	int i, byte_order = cpu->byte_order;

	if (len & MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN) {
		len &= ~MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN;
		byte_order = EMUL_LITTLE_ENDIAN;
	}

	if (byte_order == EMUL_LITTLE_ENDIAN)
		for (i=0; i<len; i++) {
			buf[i] = data & 255;
			data >>= 8;
		}
	else

14308: `if_ie.c:609` stores `pcc2_lancirq` (IEICR, 0x2a), `:612` `pcc2_lancerrirq` (0x2b), `:655`
14309: `pcc2_lancerrstat` (IEERR, 0x28) -- verified by grep in `_guest-src-openbsd22` -- plus GPIO_ICR 0x18,
14310: SCSIERR 0x2c, the printer block 0x30-0x37, SPEED 0x38 and SCCTXIACK 0x25; none has a `case`, so any
14311: access, READ included, reaches `default:` and its `exit(1)`.  Ranked FIRST, above `pcc2c040kill`, because
14312: that one needs a GENCTL write the in-tree guest never makes.  **RE-BASED 2026-09-05 (Fable design seat,
14313: verified from the tree): `if_ie` NEVER ATTACHES on the emulated mvme187** -- `machine_mvme88k.c:106` installs
14314: `unreadable` at 0xfff46000, the ie0 address, and `if_ie.c:407-409` fails its match on `badpaddr()` there, so
14315: the driver's PCC2 writes never run on this model.  The ranking rests on the RUNG-3 WITNESS and severity (one
14316: guest byte load or store to any of 43 offsets ends the host), not on the driver.  Witness (measured
14317: 2026-09-05, `regress/artifacts/pcc2defaultkill/witness_20260905/`): a GUEST `st.b` or `ld.bu` to IEICR ->
14318: exit 1 with the arm's own 'unimplemented' line first; the debugger's `put b` does NOT reach the arm.  Option set for pass 1: DECLINE + latched announce / BACKED ACCEPT (the register array
14319: already backs every offset and the memcpy read path already serves it) / FAULT.
14320: 
14321: Found by the adjudicating seat while ranking `pcc2widthkills`, and it is filed rather than
14322: folded into that round because the predicate class differs: **offset decode, not access
14323: width.**  The round-scope rule takes one site and admits a second only in the same semantic
14324: class under one fix design; these are two classes in one file.
14325: 
14326: > **The reason it matters that it has NO ROW, rather than merely being unfixed:** without one,
14327: > `pcc2widthkills`'s contract has nothing to point at when it declines to claim that wide pcc2
14328: > reads are safe.  A round that fixes the width kills and says nothing about the decode kill

14580: ### `pcc2c040kill` (HIGH) -- a LEGAL byte write to GENCTL ends the host
14581: 
14582: **2026-09-03, surfaced by the `pcc2widthkills` pass-1 panel (fable5 and kimi, independently);
14583: the panel found it while auditing the width fix and noted that "ten kills removed" would read as
14584: "file clean" unless this one is cited.**
14585: 
14586: `dev_pcc2.c`, `reassert_interrupts()` (around `:189-195`): if GENCTL has bit `0x04` set
14587: (C040), the function does `fatal("pcc2: C040 interrupt assertions... TODO"); exit(1);`.  GENCTL
14588: is written by the guest through its own arm at the LEGAL width (`ld.b`/`st.b`), and that arm
14589: calls `reassert_interrupts()` on every write.  So **a single byte store of `0x04` to
14590: `0xfff42002` ends the host** -- no width trick, no misalignment, an ordinary register write.
14591: 
14592: It was mentioned once in prose under `widthkillclass` as "a BADVAL reachable by a byte write"
14593: with no row id, which is the same gap `pcc2defaultkill` closed for the offset kill.  Filed now
14594: with an id so the width round's contract can point at it.
14595: 
14596: **ROW-DESIGN TRAP for the width round, recorded here because it is where a reader looks:**
14597: every GENCTL lane value in a width-round row must keep bit `0x04` CLEAR, or a DELETE /
14598: fall-through mutant trips THIS kill and the row dies for a reason not under test.
14599: 
14600: Witness owed (rung 3, one session): `st.b 0x04` to `0xfff42002` on the committed build ->
14601: `exit1`.  Not yet run; the width round's witness is the template.
14602: 
14603: ### `pcc2counter8` (MED) -- the counter/compare arm accepts an 8-byte write and raw-copies through neighbours
14604: 
14605: **2026-09-03, found by the codex seat on the `pcc2widthkills` pass-1 panel.**
14606: 
14607: The `T1CMP / T2CMP / T1COUNT / T2COUNT` arm does `memcpy(d->pcctwo_reg + relative_addr, data,
14608: len)` for any `len` within `PCC2_SIZE`.  The hardware guide lists 8/16/32-bit cycles only.  An
14609: 8-byte write at `T2COUNT (0x10)` raw-copies through `PSCALE (0x14)`, `PSCALEADJ (0x15)`,

codex
A one-character latch regression appears able to pass all 23 rows: the tests never revisit offset A after touching B. The changelog also claims filings and ledger updates that are absent from those files. I’m checking the remaining state and width cases before assigning the verdict.

 245: 		    just changing to it directly:  */
 246: 		timer_current_time = ( (tv.tv_usec * 0.000001 + tv.tv_sec) +
 247: 		    timer_current_time ) / 2;

 250: 		    SECONDS_BETWEEN_GETTIMEOFDAY_SYNCH);
 251: 	}

 253: 	while (timer != NULL) {
 254: 		/*
 255: 		 *  #428: bound the catch-up, and RETAIN the backlog.
 256: 		 *
 257: 		 *  This loop is the emulator's whole delivery mechanism for any timer faster
 258: 		 *  than TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY, so it must not be turned into "drop what was
 259: 		 *  missed": measured, that caps every timer at 65 Hz and a 100 Hz timer runs
 260: 		 *  at 0.65 of real time with no burst at all.  Leaving it unbounded is the
 261: 		 *  measured defect -- 20.6 million iterations in one signal after half a
 262: 		 *  second of lag, and an interval of zero never advances at all.
 263: 		 *
 264: 		 *  So: stop after TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP ticks for this timer in this signal and
 265: 		 *  leave next_tick_at where it is.  A RECOVERABLE burst then loses nothing but
 266: 		 *  its promptness -- the remainder is delivered by the signals that follow.
 267: 		 *
 268: 		 *  Said precisely, because the first draft of this comment was not: that holds
 269: 		 *  for a backlog the timer's own rate can pay off, i.e. below
 270: 		 *  TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY = 68,157,440 Hz.  Above it the debt
 271: 		 *  grows every signal and is never repaid; see timer.h, where the gap between
 272: 		 *  that figure and the domain ceiling is recorded and filed.
 273: 		 *
 274: 		 *  The hit is recorded for timer_stop() to report.  NOTHING IN THIS FILE PRINTS
 275: 		 *  FROM THE HANDLER -- which is a claim about timer.c and NOT about the handler
 276: 		 *  as a whole: a review seat traced dev_sh4.c's sh4_timer_tick(), a callback this
 277: 		 *  loop invokes, calling fatal() and exit(1) from inside SIGALRM.  That is
 278: 		 *  pre-existing and filed; it is named here so this comment is not read as
 279: 		 *  covering it.
 280: 		 */
 281: 		int budget = TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP;
 282: 
 283: 		while (timer_current_time >= timer->next_tick_at) {
 284: 			timer->timer_tick(timer, timer->extra);
 285: 			timer->next_tick_at += timer->interval;
 286: 
 287: 			if (--budget <= 0) {
 288: 				timer_catchup_hit = 1;
 289: 				break;

 291: 		}

#!/bin/bash
#  #501 pcc2defaultkill KILL TABLE -- the new default: arm of DEVICE_ACCESS(pcc2), six mutants, the 23-row
#  detector regress/pcc2_default_probe.py (frozen with the width probe it borrows session() from).
#    BASE       23/23 owed (the fix)
#    M_EXIT     (b) verbatim exit(1) reinstated
#    M_UNLATCH  (d) announce unlatched
#    M_ONELATCH (d) one whole-arm latch bit
#    M_BACKED   member 6 (a store into pcctwo_reg[]) substituted
#    M_RDKILL   (d) reads re-killed
#    M_DIRSWAP  (d) the announce's direction test inverted
set -u
G=/mnt/c/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL; SEC=$G/GXEMUL-SEC; T=/tmp/gxdk501; F=src/devices/dev_pcc2.c
OUT=/tmp/killdk501; PROBE=pcc2_default_probe.py
say() { echo "[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] $*"; }
die() { echo "*** ABORT: $*"; exit 9; }
rm -rf "$T" "$OUT"; mkdir -p "$OUT"
cp -a "$G/build" "$T" || die "clone failed"
cmp -s "$T/$F" "$SEC/$F" || { cp "$SEC/$F" "$T/$F"; say "  clone's $F refreshed from SEC (comment-only change since build/ was built)"; }
cp "$SEC/regress/$PROBE" "$OUT/frozen_probe.py" || die "cannot freeze probe"
cp "$SEC/regress/pcc2_width_probe.py" "$OUT/pcc2_width_probe.py" || die "cannot freeze the session helper source"
say "  instrument frozen: $(wc -c < "$OUT/frozen_probe.py") bytes"
build() { cd "$T" || die cd; rm -f src/devices/dev_pcc2.o; make -j4 > "$OUT/build_$1.log" 2>&1; [ "$T/gxemul" -nt "$T/$F" ] || die "$1: binary not newer than source"; }
score() {
    cd "$SEC/regress" || die cd
    python3 -u "$OUT/frozen_probe.py" "$T/gxemul" "$1" > "$OUT/$1.out" 2>&1
    grep -q PCC2D_PROBE_DONE "$OUT/$1.out" || die "$1: NO COMPLETION SENTINEL"
    say "  $1 [$2]: $(grep -m1 PCC2D_RESULT "$OUT/$1.out")"
    grep -m1 PCC2D_RED "$OUT/$1.out" | sed 's/PCC2D_RED=/      RED: /' || true
}
mut() {
    echo "$F  mutant $1" > "$T/.MUTANT"; cp "$SEC/$F" "$T/$F"
    python3 - "$T/$F" "$3" <<'PYEOF'
import io, sys
p, body = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]; s = io.open(p, encoding='utf-8').read()
ns = {'s': s}; exec(body, ns); io.open(p, 'w', encoding='utf-8', newline='\n').write(ns['s'])
PYEOF
    [ $? -eq 0 ] || die "$1: mutator failed"
    cmp -s "$T/$F" "$SEC/$F" && die "$1: file UNCHANGED after mutation"
    MD=$(diff "$SEC/$F" "$T/$F" | grep -c "^[<>]"); say "    [$1] mutant diff vs SEC: $MD lines; first added: $(diff "$SEC/$F" "$T/$F" | grep "^>" | head -1 | cut -c1-100)"; [ "$MD" -gt 0 ] || die "$1: MUTATION NOT APPLIED (source identical to SEC)"
    build "$1"
    say "    [$1] binary $(date -r "$T/gxemul" +%H:%M:%S)  source $(date -r "$T/$F" +%H:%M:%S)"
    score "$1" "$2"
}
say "BASE -- 23/23 owed"; build BASE; score BASE CONTROL
BASE_RES=$(grep -m1 PCC2D_RESULT "$OUT/BASE.out" | cut -d= -f2)
[ "$BASE_RES" = "23/23" ] || die "BASE IS NOT FULL ($BASE_RES): $(grep -m1 PCC2D_RED "$OUT/BASE.out") -- a table on a broken instrument measures nothing (tablebasenotfull)"
say "RESTORE from SEC + REBUILD + verify, THEN clear the sentinel"

NO-GO

The process-exit removal is correct. Closure is blocked by an inaccurate read diagnostic, an escaping latch mutation, and records that claim unfinished work is complete.

1. **The read announcement can report a different byte from the response.** At [dev_pcc2.c:693](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_pcc2.c:693), the diagnostic reloads `pcctwo_reg[relative_addr]` after the response was copied. Counter interiors reach this arm, and [timer writeback](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_pcc2.c:259) runs through the [SIGALRM callback](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/timer.c:284). A tick between those reads can make the announced byte differ from the returned byte. This is a source-derived interleaving, not a runtime measurement.

   Wider reads expose another problem: the message reports only the first byte without identifying it as such. For example, the initialized halfword beginning at PSCALECNT contains `00 df`, while the announcement says `served 0x00`. The end-span path also prepares zeroes instead of copying register storage.

   Log the prepared response, state its width—or explicitly identify the first returned byte—and preserve the zero-fill distinction. Add a numerical diagnostic assertion: [the existing `named rd srvd` row](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/pcc2_default_probe.py:101) counts only `"served 0x"`.

2. **Deleting one character from the latch update escapes all 23 rows.** Change `|=` to `=` at [the latch update](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_pcc2.c:687). That forgets earlier offsets whenever a new offset announces.

   `latch sameoff` still sees one announcement for A,A; `latch twooff` still sees two for A,B; `named wr msg` still sees eighteen because every offset is distinct. The write/read pair also remains green. None revisits A after B.

   A `latch aba` row must expect **2** announcements for A,B,A; this mutation produces **3**. I checked that distinction with a finite latch model. The predicted **23/23 escape has not been measured in the emulator**. The width probe already documents this same mutation class, making its omission here particularly difficult to justify.

The remaining mechanics check out: the unsigned address is folded into `0..63`, making both shifts defined; initialization zeroes the complete allocated structure; `warned` and `warned_unimpl` occupy separate fields; `fatal()` prints without terminating; and the default arm’s `break` reaches `return 1`. The new arm does not alter the prepared read buffer or store into the register array.

For the supplied OpenBSD source, the Ethernet match fails before its PCC2-writing attach path. Existing cased accesses remain unchanged. However, **“byte accesses only” is false**: [clock.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/_guest-src-openbsd22/sys/arch/mvme88k/dev/clock.c:236) accesses the `u_long` counter/compare fields too. Also, lines 561–564 are the caller’s conditional return when `ie_setupram()` fails; they are not that function’s implementation.

The detector distinguishes the particular backed-store mutant at two zero-initialized offsets. Its results do not establish preservation of arbitrary existing state, repeated-write disposition, or wide-write disposition.

That is **4/4 execution cells**, not complete state coverage. The missing state is **target bit set while another offset’s bit is also set**. No existing sequence tests whether previously accumulated bits survive touching another offset. Repeated reads and read-then-write sequences are also absent.

The switch’s cased/default outcomes are both exercised. There is no newly added value/range predicate inside the default arm; the modulo and read-span predicate are inherited dependencies. Nevertheless, the announced contract includes widths the detector never drives at default offsets. Deriving coverage from `NAMED18`, `INWORD`, and `UNNAMED` gives:

- Default writes at **27/43 distinct byte starting offsets**.
- Default reads at **18/43**, all in the named population.
- **No wide default transactions**.
- No two-instance fixture, as already acknowledged.

Add named measurements for `latch aba`, nonzero read preservation with exact announced-value comparison, and wide-write preservation before and after latching. Otherwise explicitly retain those gaps in the closure denominator.

Its seat evidence points to pass 1.
